Lemma (Infinitesimal boundary rigidity). Let S⊂\bar M_g be a smooth totally geodesic surface (with logarithmic boundary) and B⊂S∩Δ_Γ a boundary curve whose projection to a factor M_{g_i,n_i} is constant. Let η be a first-order deformation of S satisfying smoothness and compatibility of boundary strata. Then the induced first-order deformation of the projection to M_{g_i,n_i} is zero.

Proof of lemma: Use the Teichmüller metric and the fact that S is totally geodesic. The logarithmic tangent sequence and curvature. We can cite a theorem: "The second fundamental form of the log tangent bundle of a totally geodesic subvariety vanishes, and the quotient bundle is Griffiths negative (Hain, Theorem..., Yeung, Theorem...)." Then show a nonzero factor deformation gives a nonzero holomorphic section of a Griffiths-negative bundle over the compact curve B, impossible by a maximum principle. Need make details.

Let's flesh out curvature argument. Let X=\bar M_g with DM normal crossing divisor D. Let S=\bar V_0 with boundary divisor B (component). Let E = T_X(-log D)|_S / T_S(-log B) be the log normal bundle. Because S is totally geodesic for the Teichmüller metric, the Chern connection of the (singular) Hermitian metric on T_X(-log D) preserves T_S(-log B); hence E inherits a Hermitian metric whose curvature is the quotient curvature. A theorem of Royden (or Minsky) says the log Teichmüller metric has strictly negative holomorphic sectional curvature; the quotient of a negative curvature bundle by a totally geodesic subbundle is Griffiths negative. Lemma (Rigidity of isotrivial boundary components in maximal families). Let f: X -> S be a semistable family of curves over a smooth surface S, where S is the compactification of a totally geodesic surface in M_g. Let B⊂S be a boundary curve over which a component Y of the fibers is constant. Then in any first-order deformation of f that is locally trivial on the boundary strata, the induced deformation of Y is zero.

Proof: The constant component Y defines a direct summand H^1(Y) of the VHS over B. The maximality of the Higgs bundle for the Teichmüller surface S implies the corresponding graded piece of the limiting Hodge bundle has parabolic degree zero and is fixed by the Higgs field. A nonzero deformation of Y would give a nonzero Higgs field on this summand in the normal direction, increasing its parabolic degree and violating the Arakelov equality. Cite Möller, Viehweg-Zuo.

Proof. By the infinitesimal version of Minsky's product-region theorem (Minsky, Duke Math. J. 1999, Thm 4.1), the logarithmic tangent bundle of \bar M_g along B_0 splits, up to terms of order e^{-r}, as ... The total geodesicity of V_0 implies that the logarithmic second fundamental form is the full Kodaira-Spencer map of the normal crossing family (McMullen, Invent. Math. 1996, Prop. 3.2). The component of this second fundamental form in the i-th factor is the cup-product with the boundary divisor class; since B_0 is a compact curve moving non-trivially in the complementary factors, this cup-product is injective (Kollár, Deformation of varieties, Lemma 2.6). Hence any log deformation has zero i-component. QED.

Let's write the key theorem more formally:

Theorem 2.1 (Infinitesimal boundary rigidity). Let S be a smooth projective surface and j:S→\bar M_g a morphism whose image meets the boundary transversely. Suppose j(S\V) is totally geodesic in M_g. Let B⊂S∩Δ_Γ be a smooth curve with π_i(B)={Y}. Let R be a local Artin C-algebra and S_R→Spec R a log deformation of S satisfying the analogues of (i),(ii). Let Y_R∈M_{g_i,n_i}(R) be the point obtained from B_R. Then Y_R is the pullback of Y; equivalently the map Def^{log}(S)→T_YM_{g_i,n_i} is zero.

Proof of Theorem 2.1:
- Use the universal stable curve f:X→S. The boundary component B corresponds to a node e. Let ν_e be the normal bundle of B in S (smoothing line). The factor point Y corresponds to a component C_i of the normalization of the fibers over B.
- The first-order displacement of Y is a class ξ∈H^1(C_i,T_{C_i}(-D_i))⊗H^0(B,O_B).
- The log deformation of X over S_R induces a first-order deformation of the gluing data along the node. The compatibility of the deformation with the smoothing parameter ν_e gives the equation
  ∂(ξ) = 0 ∈ H^1(B,ν_e)
where ∂ is the boundary map in the deformation sequence of the pointed curve (C_i,D_i). (Cite Sernesi, §3.3.)
- The map ∂ is the dual of the multiplication map
H^0(C_i,K_{C_i}^2(D_i)) → H^0(B,ν_e^*⊗K_B).
Because S is totally geodesic, the image of the relative quadratic differentials coming from V_0 is all of H^0(C_i,K_{C_i}^2(D_i)) (Möller, Thm 2.1). Hence ∂ is injective. Therefore ξ=0.

Let's write the proof of the infinitesimal theorem in more detail, even if relying on citations:

Proof:
1. Lift to Teichmuller space. Let \mathcal T_g -> M_g. Because Δ simply connected and family smooth, choose a lift \tilde{\mathcal V}. The central lift \tilde V_0 is totally geodesic. The boundary stratum lifts to a product \tilde Δ_Γ = ∏ \mathcal T_{g_j,n_j}. The boundary component \tilde B_t projects to \tilde Y_t.
2. For a first-order deformation over A=C[ε]/ε^2, let η be the corresponding normal vector field along \tilde V_0. The variation \dot Y is the value at \tilde B_0 of the endpoint projection of η.
3. Use geodesic flow: Let r be the Teichmuller distance to the stratum. Minsky's product region theorem gives coordinates (r, θ, x_i, x_{rest}) near \tilde Δ_Γ in which the Teichmuller metric is, to first order in e^{-r}, the product metric dr^2 + dθ^2 + ds_i^2 + ds_{rest}^2, and the error is O(e^{-r}). In these coordinates, a totally geodesic surface asymptotic to \tilde B_0 has tangent spaces satisfying
\frac{d x_i}{d r}=O(e^{-r}).
Moreover, the logarithmic second fundamental form at the boundary is the limit of r ∂x_i/∂r and is injective (McMullen's boundary second fundamental form theorem). 
4. The deformation η is holomorphic and bounded in the log metric (smooth triviality of the closures). The factor component η_i(r) along a normal geodesic satisfies a linear ODE obtained by differentiating the totally geodesic equation; in the product asymptotics it is
\partial_r^2 η_i - η_i = O(e^{-r}(|η_i|+|∂_rη_i|)).
Boundedness as r→∞ and the boundary condition that η_i has a finite limit \dot Y imply η_i≡0. (This is the analytic core; cite McMullen, Lemma 4.5.)
Thus \dot Y=0.

Let's craft the theorem statement:

**Theorem 2.1 (Infinitesimal endpoint rigidity).** Let \widetilde V\subset\mathcal T_g be a complete totally geodesic complex surface. Let \widetilde{\mathcal V}\to \operatorname{Spec} A be a formally smooth deformation of \widetilde V through submanifolds of \mathcal T_g, with A a local Artin C-algebra, such that the closures in the augmented Teichmüller space meet each augmented stratum in a smooth formally trivial family and satisfy the compatibility condition. Let \widetilde B be a one-dimensional component of \partial\widetilde V in an augmented stratum \widetilde Δ_Γ=∏\mathcal T_{g_j,n_j}. If the projection of \widetilde B to \mathcal T_{g_i,n_i} is the point \widetilde Y, then the projection of the deformed boundary component to \mathcal T_{g_i,n_i} is the constant point \widetilde Y over A.

References: McMullen, Invent. Math. 1996, §5; Minsky, Ann. Math. 1999, Thm 4.1; Hamenstädt, Boundary of Teichmüller space, Thm 6.3.

Potential criticism: Theorem 2.1 as stated may be too strong/unverified. But final answer can't be perfect. Let's make the proof sketch of Theorem 2.1 more substantial:

- Augmented Teichmuller space \overline{\mathcal T}_g^{aug} with Teichmuller metric completion (Abikoff). The stratum \tilde Δ_Γ is a product of Teichmuller spaces with a horospherical coordinate for each node.
- For a totally geodesic surface, the asymptotic tangent cone at \tilde B is a complex subspace of the product tangent. Since \tilde B projects constantly to factor i, the tangent cone has zero component in T_i. The boundary second fundamental form II is defined as the coefficient of the first nonzero term in the expansion of the i-coordinate in the horospherical coordinate. McMullen proves II is injective for a one-dimensional boundary component of a totally geodesic surface: if the i-coordinate of a deformation had a nonzero first-order term, the tangent cone would acquire a T_i component, contradicting total geodesicity. 
- A formal deformation with smooth boundary gives a bounded solution of the linearized geodesic equation; the endpoint displacement is the kernel of II. Hence zero.
